### 2026-11-15 — scan-rune world-create click was one pixel outside the real hitbox

- Inspected the actual screenshot artifact from [run #43749075503](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43749075503), artifact [14175364151](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43749075503/artifacts/14175364151). The files 04-before-world-create.png and 04a-after-create-world-click.png are 1280x800 root captures. The X11 Minecraft window geometry logged by the runner is 1024x768 at root offset (1,20).
- The visible Create New World button occupies root pixels approximately x=205..502, y=718..756. The attempted click was root (349,757) (LOCAL=348,737): X was inside the button, but Y was one pixel below its bottom edge. The after-click screenshot still shows the world-creation screen; no player-login event followed. This is the first exact, screenshot-backed explanation for INTEGRATED_WORLD_PLAYER_LOGIN=NOT_CONFIRMED.
- The prior note incorrectly compared window-local y=737 directly with root screenshot bounds and therefore accepted the bad 96% coordinate. Corrected the generator to target root (349,738) using Y + HEIGHT * 935 / 1000; the regression now explicitly transforms local coordinates to root coordinates and rejects the old 96% click (349,757). The original shared 93% formula would have produced root y=734 and was already inside the observed hitbox; the 96% override introduced the miss.
- No production Java changes. Exact-head Python/Bash checks and a new real-client run are required before claiming the world-login blocker is cleared. Progress remains 59/100; no gameplay acceptance is inferred from this test-driver correction.

### 2026-11-15 — non-blocking crystal support reads during chunk lighting

- Re-inspected the exact native scan-rune failure [#43758357833](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43758357833), artifact [14179608284](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43758357833/artifacts/14179608284). In the captured game JVM dump, the `light` worker is in `ThreadedLevelLightEngine` → `BlockBehaviour.getLightBlock` → `CrystalClusterBlock.getShape` → `hasCrystalSupport` → `Level.getBlockState` → `ServerChunkCache.getChunk`, waiting on a chunk future. At the same time, the server thread is inside `CrystalClusterWorldGen.generateClusterAt` → `Level.setBlock` → `NeighborUpdater` → `ServerChunkCache.getChunk`, also waiting. This is concrete evidence of a cross-thread chunk-future wait in the crystal placement/lighting path; it does not by itself prove every failed client run has this cause.
- The subsequent exact-head scan-rune run [#43760481267](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43760481267) did reach `Dev joined the game`, then stopped recording accepted commands after `/time set noon` and `/weather clear`; `/forceload` and target checks were only shown as typed by X11. Thus world-login is intermittent and the test-driver command path remains a separate unresolved blocker.
- Production fix: server-side support checks now use `ServerChunkCache.getChunkNow` and read the returned `LevelChunk` directly. A chunk that is not yet fully available is treated as unsupported for that query. The client-side outline path is unchanged. This avoids `Level.getBlockState` synchronously joining a pending server chunk load from the lighting worker.
- Regression: the first real NeoForge background-worker assertion exposed that `getChunkNow` also returns no chunk from the worker (the main-thread boundary assertion still passed); therefore that first test failed with a full-cube fallback. The follow-up below moves the off-thread guard to `getShape` itself and verifies an empty, non-world-reading shape on the lighting worker while preserving the exact lower-half outline on the server thread.
- Files changed in this iteration: `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/block/CrystalClusterBlock.java`, `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/gametest/CrystalSpreadGameTests.java`, and this lane journal.
- Acceptance is pending exact-head Aura/Crystal GameTests, full NeoForge/JAR/dedicated-server CI, and a fresh native scan-rune run. No score increase: fixed audit remains 59/100 (M08 5/9, M16 4/6). No main or other workstream branch is changed; PR #4 remains unmerged.


### 2026-11-15 — off-thread crystal shape must not touch ServerLevel

- Exact-head GameTest [#43762629086](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43762629086) ran on commit `100bcc365dc236651a646759d54be9604353d6cb`. The existing server-thread boundary outline still passed, but the new background-worker assertion failed: it received the defensive full-cube bounds instead of the lower half. This shows `getChunkNow` is not a usable off-thread read path in this fixture; do not claim the first fix solved the lighting-worker wait.
- Corrective production change: `CrystalClusterBlock.getShape` now detects a `ServerLevel` query off the server thread and returns `Shapes.empty()` before any neighbour lookup. The registered crystals already use `noOcclusion()` and light level 1, so this fallback is for the threaded lighting calculation only. Server-thread placement/outline and client render queries still use the BETA26 six-face support calculation; server-thread support reads continue to use non-loading `getChunkNow` + direct `LevelChunk` access.
- Regression now asserts that a background-worker shape query completes within 5 seconds, returns the empty non-world-reading fallback, and leaves the east chunk unloaded; the existing server-thread assertion still requires the BETA26 lower half-cube. This explicitly tests the two distinct contracts rather than pretending worker-thread access has the same world view.
- Production correction committed as [`0c3833e`](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/0c3833ebbc51ba3138ef62ff65a9448f1c60686d). Exact-head push **Thaumcraft Port CI #43762920036 PASS**: NeoForge build, dedicated-server start (`Done (9.121s)`) and JAR artifact [14181365606](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43762920036/artifacts/14181365606). Exact-head push **Aura/Crystal GameTests #43762920053 PASS**: seven GameTests completed in 1.571 s and 927.9 ms; both the server-thread lower-half outline and `TC_GAMETEST_CRYSTAL_OUTLINE_OFF_THREAD=PASS no_world_lookup=YES no_force_load=YES` were logged. PR-event GameTest #43762924978 is still in progress; native full-client #43762920048 and crystal-outline client #43762924807 are still in progress; scan-rune client #43762924908 is queued. The push GameTest proves the off-thread shape contract, not yet the full native scan-rune/gameplay acceptance. No score change; M08 remains 5/9 and total 59/100.

### Evidence received before changing code

All 19 PR-triggered workflows associated with the prior code HEAD `13fb4c9c` completed successfully:
- Aura/crystal native GameTests: 8/8 required tests passed in both cold-restart phases. The read phase logged `TC_GAMETEST_AURA_COLD_RESTART_QUEUE_SKIP=PASS existing_record_preserved=YES gaussian=0 local_target_pending=NO`; the persisted Base/Vis/Flux values survived the two separate JVMs.
- Full Port CI: Core parity, original-resource checks, NeoForge build, dedicated-server smoke and JAR upload all passed.
- Arcane Workbench real-client matrix: RU/EN at GUI scales 2 and 3, 4/4 passed.
- Crystal outline real-client smoke: all six support faces and the multi-face full-cube case passed.
- Other M16 client regressions passed: Salis unload/reload and ticket cleanup, original label art/tint, readable GUI text, Thaumonomicon page sound, Curio learn sound, Thaumometer scan sound/runes, crystal place/break sound, tube particles, Salis dust/Nitor particles, jar interaction/SoundType.

These results validate the prior HEAD only; they do not validate the new change below.

### Source discrepancy found

Pinned BETA26 `BlockUtils.isBlockTouching(IBlockAccess, BlockPos, Material, boolean)` iterates all six `EnumFacing` neighbors and reads each neighbor's block state and (when requested) side solidity. `ThaumcraftWorldGenerator.generateOres` uses that predicate while placing the 3×3×3 crystal cluster.

The port's deferred `CrystalClusterWorldGen.PendingQueue` previously waited only for the center chunk. Its `touchesRock` adapter deliberately skips support positions in unloaded chunks to avoid joining/force-loading them. At a horizontal chunk edge this could turn an actually valid rock support in an unloaded cardinal neighbor into a false negative; the one-shot worldgen request would then be consumed and the missed cluster would not be retried.

### Implemented

The production queue now waits until the center chunk and all four cardinal neighbors are fully available via the existing non-joining `ThaumcraftAuraWorldEvents.fiveChunksLoaded` check. No neighbor is force-loaded. The check is necessary because cluster candidate cells remain inside their own chunk, while one of their horizontal support faces can lie in the adjacent chunk.

Native GameTest added to the existing eight-test suite:
- creates a remote center chunk with an unloaded west neighbor;
- proves queue processing invokes no generator and does not increase loaded-chunk count;
- loads the cardinal neighborhood;
- places a stone support in the west neighbor;
- resumes the same queue entry and invokes the production `generateClusterAt` path with deterministic RNG;
- verifies an actual Aer crystal is placed on the border cell.

The shell runner now requires `TC_GAMETEST_CRYSTAL_WORLDGEN_BORDER_SUPPORT=PASS no_force_load=YES deferred=1 resumed=1 crystal_placed=YES` in both cold-restart phases. Core parity also checks that the production adapter uses the five-chunk gate.

- `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/world/CrystalClusterWorldGen.java`
- `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/world/AuraGenerationGameTests.java`
- `src/test/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/port/core/CrystalGrowthParityTest.java`
- `tools/run_crystal_spread_gametest.sh`

Implementation commit: `eaad92a877f11270693d9bfdea111eed58aa75eb`.
Deterministic fixture refinement: `237890b3e83f1e0329054d5cc96f5156d64ee11d`.

## 2026-11-16 — Salis positive-control packet was outside vanilla delivery radius

- Fresh baseline: main `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; assigned branch before this change `ddd125e45cac59384b2e789954aacc02723a2870`; PR #4 remained Draft/Open/Unmerged. Re-read current `AGENTS.md`, `ROADMAP_RU.md`, `analysis/PARALLEL_WORKFLOW_RU.md`, fixed audit and this lane. Compared all other open PRs #1–#3 and #5–#18 (including M12 PR #9): none changes the four files in this correction.
- Inspected the failed real-client artifact [#14220982312](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43854168610/artifacts/14220982312) from [run #43854168610](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43854168610). The actual server error is `outbound recorder did not observe its positive-control particle packet`; Nitor's real client tick passed. The same failure is present in the earlier artifact [#14217406470](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43848739099/artifacts/14217406470).
- Root cause is the fixture geometry, not a proven Netty recorder or production queue defect. The test actor was left at approximately `(2.5, 150, 11.5)`, while the remote origin is `(8207.5, 100.5, 8200.5)`. Production `SalisSparkleTrailQueue.emit` calls `ServerLevel.sendParticles(player, particle, false, ...)`; the 1.21.1 API documents that this viewer-specific path sends only when the player is near, with a 32-block threshold when `force=false` (see [ServerLevel 1.21.1 mappings](https://mappings.dev/1.21.1/net/minecraft/server/level/ServerLevel.html)). The probe counted the server-side invocation but then incorrectly expected a packet for a viewer thousands of blocks away.
- Corrected only the opt-in probe fixture: it saves the actor's exact position/yaw/pitch, teleports the actor to the already-forced remote origin for the production positive-control send, asserts the actor is within the vanilla 32-block radius, waits for the real outbound packet recorder, then restores the actor before queueing the delayed effect and releasing the fixture ticket. Failure cleanup also attempts to restore the actor. No production particle behavior or shared registry changed.
- Added ordered real-client markers for actor move/return and strengthened `tools/test_salis_chunk_probe_cleanup.py` so the fixture cannot silently revert to an out-of-range positive control. Changed paths: `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/item/SalisSparkleChunkUnloadProbe.java`, `tools/client_dust_sound_smoke.py`, `tools/test_salis_chunk_probe_cleanup.py`, and this lane journal.
- This is a test-fixture correction, not a gameplay feature or audit-point claim. New exact-head static Python regression, native real-client smoke, and full NeoForge CI are pending. Local Git/Gradle/Minecraft remains unavailable because this runtime cannot resolve `github.com`; no local build or game run is claimed. Fixed audit remains **59/100**, M08 **5/9**, M16 **4/6**.
- Next: inspect the exact-head run for `SALIS_NATIVE_ACTOR_MOVED_TO_PACKET_ORIGIN`, exactly one outbound packet, `SALIS_NATIVE_ACTOR_RETURNED_FROM_PACKET_ORIGIN`, then native unload/reload cancellation and ticket cleanup. Do not move on to another M16 sound or M08 feature until this probe passes or its next concrete failure is diagnosed.

## 2026-11-16 — defer crystal removal while a support-neighbour chunk is unavailable

- Fresh baseline: main `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; own branch HEAD before this change `031bea27ab185c85eb9c7dafa3a253dd61b9e811`; PR #4 remains Draft/Open/Unmerged. Re-read main `AGENTS.md`, `ROADMAP_RU.md`, `analysis/PARALLEL_WORKFLOW_RU.md`, the fixed audit and this lane. Compared the six candidate paths against every other currently open PR (#1–#3 and #5–#19); no path overlap was found. No common registry, Gradle file, workflow definition, global localization, or foreign branch is changed.
- Pinned BETA26 `BlockCrystal.neighborChanged` immediately drops the crystal when `BlockUtils.isBlockTouching(... Material.ROCK ...)` is false. The modern port deliberately uses non-joining chunk reads, but its scheduled `tick` treated a missing/in-flight neighbour chunk as proof that no support exists. A border crystal could therefore be dropped while a possible support face was merely unavailable.
- Added a three-state decision: remove only when all six adjacent support positions are in fully loaded chunks and none supplies a valid support face; retain when a support is found; defer and schedule a bounded 20-tick retry when the result is unknown. All availability checks use `getChunkNow`; no neighbour is force-loaded.
- Extended the existing native Aura/Crystal GameTest with an actual server-world sequence: unavailable east chunk → crystal retained without drops or force-load → east stone support loaded and crystal retained → support removed with all neighbours loaded → crystal removed and exactly one crystal item dropped. Core parity covers the decision truth table and adapter contract.
- Changed files: `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/block/CrystalClusterBlock.java`, new `src/main/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/port/core/LegacyCrystalSupportLogic.java`, `src/test/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/port/core/CrystalGrowthParityTest.java`, `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/gametest/CrystalSpreadGameTests.java`, `tools/run_crystal_spread_gametest.sh`, and this lane journal.
- Exact-head Core/NeoForge and Aura/Crystal GameTests have not run yet. Local Git/Gradle/Minecraft is unavailable because this runtime cannot resolve `github.com`; no local test is claimed. Fixed audit remains **59/100**, M08 **5/9**, M16 **4/6**; no score awarded.
- Next: inspect exact-head Full Port CI and both Aura/Crystal GameTest phases. If the new marker sequence passes, keep the safety adaptation documented and continue M08 only with another independent, source-backed world behavior.

## 2026-11-16 — Salis actor-session logout cleanup and recipient parity

- Compared the port against the pinned BETA26 source reference [ItemMagicDust.java at 954022b](https://github.com/TheDarkTower314/Thaumcraft-6-Source-Code/blob/954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81/src/main/java/thaumcraft/common/items/resources/ItemMagicDust.java). The original doSparkles method is called only inside the world.isRemote branch, so the dust trail is client-local to the player using Salis. The port's ServerLevel.sendParticles(ServerPlayer, ...) overload is the corresponding actor-only recipient path; a broadcast would be a parity regression.
- Found a lifecycle gap: a delayed Pending entry retained the exact ServerPlayer reference after logout until its due tick. Existing due-time guards prevented a packet to a departed/reconnected session, but did not release the queued reference immediately.
- Added PlayerEvent.PlayerLoggedOutEvent cleanup. It removes entries only when both the ServerPlayer object and UUID match, so a new connection reusing the UUID is not cancelled accidentally. Added a per-actor pending-count observer for regression assertions.
- Extended the JVM API-double test: an actual logout event removes the pending entry before its due tick; same-UUID replacement remains isolated; a second connected ServerPlayer receives no packet when the first actor triggers the effect. This is a JVM recipient-contract test, not a two-real-client network test.
- Extended the opt-in real-client probe: after the existing native unload/reload/Netty-recorder/ticket checks, it queues one delayed actor-bound effect, disconnects the real integrated-client player, and requires the logout event to remove that entry before the final SALIS_NATIVE_CHUNK_UNLOAD_ACCEPTED marker. Server-stop fallback checks the logout marker rather than allowing shutdown cleanup to mask a missing logout handler.
- Code/test commits: [5a028f7](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/5a028f73b564592a7943098c4f4092fc4f5cc8f3), [8488e0b](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/8488e0b55548335da8f567c47c6476c9988d910f), [162d7ce](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/162d7ce59eb0ba12d26e6264cff7df70adcf171f), [01f69d5](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/01f69d54298d79e63aa9d732a9963eadce86c602), [77fd037](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/77fd037ce7bb0b97062eea56398ba3973e224cc9).

## 2026-11-17 — BETA26 Flux Rift segment entity effects

- Fresh baseline before this iteration: main `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; closure branch `edafe6096737a8793c1af61fdcd2abf7d0023ffc`; PR #4 Draft/Open/Unmerged. Re-read main AGENTS, ROADMAP, parallel workflow and the fixed 59/100 audit. Enumerated all open PRs #1–#27 and checked the four intended paths against every other PR (#1–#3 and #5–#27): no path overlap. No shared registry, Gradle, CI workflow, main or foreign branch changed.
- Source: pinned BETA26 `EntityFluxRift.onUpdate()` at `954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81`. Every server tick, it chooses `rand.nextInt(points.size() - 1)`, queries entities in an axis-aligned cube with half-extent 0.5 around that polycone point, skips creative players, applies 2.0 OUT_OF_WORLD damage to remaining live entities, and removes Item entities. The original `EntityUtils.getEntitiesInRange` confirms the query is an AABB expansion, not a spherical-distance test.
- Implemented that entity-target slice in `FluxRiftEntity.tick()`: preserve the source RNG point selection, use the same 0.5 AABB, skip the Rift itself and creative players, apply the 2.0 OUT_OF_WORLD damage source, and discard item entities. Per-target exception isolation mirrors the original catch. In Mojang mappings for 1.21.1 the API method is `DamageSources.fellOutOfWorld()` (Yarn calls the same source `outOfWorld()`); the initial compile attempt exposed this naming difference, and the production call is now corrected to the Mojang name. Block ray-trace/destruction, growth, weighted events, collapse, rewards and research unlock are still not implemented.
- Extended the existing nine-test Native GameTest rather than adding a tenth registration. It places one pig and one item entity at each eligible geometry point, invokes the actual production `rift.tick()`, and requires at least one living target to lose health and at least one item entity to be removed. The fail-closed runner now requires `TC_GAMETEST_FLUX_RIFT_SEGMENT_ENTITY_EFFECTS=PASS damaged=YES items=REMOVED`.
- Changed paths: `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/entity/FluxRiftEntity.java`, `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/world/AuraGenerationGameTests.java`, `tools/run_crystal_spread_gametest.sh`, and this lane journal.
- Prior exact-code acceptance: Aura/Crystal GameTests [#44015594070](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44015594070) **SUCCESS** on `3a0c7e70f7c43fd78075c3b6d015a8577d3c9d72`, including write phase, 120-tick decay, ticket release and separate-process cold-restart read; Full Port CI [#44015594059](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44015594059) **SUCCESS** on the same code SHA. The current branch HEAD `edafe6096737a8793c1af61fdcd2abf7d0023ffc` is journal-only beyond that functional tree. These prior runs do not test this new entity-effect implementation.
- New exact-head Native GameTest and Full Port CI are pending. No score change: fixed audit **59/100**, M08 **5/9**, M16 **4/6**. Next: inspect exact-head GameTest marker and full NeoForge/server CI. If they pass, proceed to the separate BETA26 block-raytrace/destruction branch; if not, fix the first concrete failure. No category closure or score award is claimed.

## 2026-11-17 — BETA26 Flux Rift segment block ray trace

- Fresh baseline: main `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; own branch `b003c86d377f6b9b525bf83e73c76106530c467e` (1086 ahead / 0 behind); PR #4 Draft/Open/Unmerged. - Source: pinned BETA26 `EntityFluxRift.onUpdate()`, commit `954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81`: for the same random adjacent point pair used by entity effects, `world.rayTraceBlocks(v1, v2, false)`; if a block is hit, require non-air, hardness `>= 0.0F`, and `canCollideCheck(state, false)`; emit world event 2001 with the block-state ID and replace the block with air. The `false` argument means fluids are not ray-trace targets. ## 2026-11-17 — BETA26 Flux Rift periodic growth

- Fresh main remains `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; current own branch baseline `51be637134c543c3d67d127b15fe9e8fbf26fb44`; PR #4 remains Draft/Open/Unmerged. Checked changed paths against all other open PRs #1–#3 and #5–#30: no overlap on the Flux Rift entity, its Native GameTest, the lane-specific GameTest runner or this journal.
- Source: pinned BETA26 `EntityFluxRift.onUpdate()` at `954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81`. Every 600 entity ticks at the entity-ID phase, the original reads local Flux, computes `sqrt(riftSize * 2)`, and if Flux is at least that cost, size is below 100 and stability is not `VERY_STABLE`, drains that amount and increases size by one. Original stability classification is `VERY_STABLE` only when stability is strictly greater than 50.0; growth follows the 120-tick stability decay and the collapse branch in the same server update.
- Production adds the same entity-specific 600-tick gate and an all-or-nothing aura debit before incrementing size. It intentionally does not implement the following weighted instability-event roll in this slice. No shared registry, Gradle, global CI, main or foreign feature branch changed.
- Native GameTest extends the existing collapse test (no extra test registration): a real registered entity grows on its scheduled phase at the exact `sqrt(10)` Flux threshold; off-phase ticks do not debit; insufficient Flux does not partially debit; stability 50.0 remains eligible while 50.01 is blocked; size 100 is capped without debit. The runner requires the new growth marker in the write phase; the independent-process read phase remains unchanged.
- Code and server evidence are pending exact-head Actions. Fixed audit remains **59/100**, M08 **5/9**; no point awarded and no category closed. Next M08 slice after exact-head verification: weighted instability-event selection and the smallest self-contained event whose required entity/item foundations already exist; original void-seed drops and nearby collapse effects remain deferred because the void-seed item/effect foundations are not yet registered.


### 2026-11-17 — growth GameTest fixture correction

- The first exact-head Aura/Crystal run [#44053612303](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44053612303) **FAILED** at the scheduled-growth assertion before emitting the growth marker. Server log: `advancesbeta26collapseandaurapulses failed ... BETA26 scheduled Flux growth must debit sqrt(2*size) and add one size`. Other aura/crystal and Rift segment markers before that point passed. This is not yet evidence of a production growth failure: the test helper called `Entity.tick()` directly and incorrectly assumed that `super.tick()` increments `tickCount`.
- Minecraft 1.21.1 increments `Entity.tickCount` in the level's `commonTick` before calling `Entity.tick()`; the real production schedule therefore sees the post-commonTick value. The focused test now explicitly sets that value before its direct `tick()` call, and includes tick/id/phase/size/Flux/stability in any future assertion failure. Source/API reference: https://minecraftdocs.dev/systems/entities/entity-anatomy.
- Production `FluxRiftEntity.java` was not changed in this correction. A new exact-head Native GameTest and Full Port CI run are required before accepting the growth behavior. Score remains **59/100**, M08 **5/9**; PR #4 remains Draft/Open/Unmerged.

## 2026-11-17 — six-face Essentia Mirror bounds test: compile blocker repaired

- Fresh main: `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`. Own branch `feature/closure-near-complete`; PR #4 remains Open/Draft/Unmerged. The branch was 1106 commits ahead and 0 behind main before this journal-only update.
- Exact-head CI on prior code/test HEAD `d6cad13b3565e9a669ec201f40b47b92ec81a045` exposed a real Java compile error at `AuraGenerationGameTests.java:101`: calling `BlockBehaviour.getCollisionShape(BlockState, BlockGetter, BlockPos, CollisionContext)` through the block instance is illegal because that override point is protected. The same single compile error caused the Aura/Crystal GameTest and several M16 client workflows to fail before their gameplay steps. It was not a runtime collision mismatch.
- Fixed the call to use the public `BlockState.getCollisionShape(BlockGetter, BlockPos, CollisionContext)` API, which is the mapped 1.21.1 state-level accessor (see [1.21.1 mappings](https://mappings.dev/1.21.1/net/minecraft/world/level/block/state/BlockBehaviour%24BlockStateBase.html)). The production mirror block was not changed.
- Hardened the acceptance: `essentiaMirrorUsesBETA26FaceBounds` is now a required GameTest, and `tools/run_crystal_spread_gametest.sh` requires `TC_GAMETEST_MIRROR_FACE_BOUNDS=PASS facings=6 thickness=2/16 collision=YES` in both independent write/read phases. Expected required-test count is now 12 in every pass/shutdown gate; an optional or unexecuted test can no longer be silently ignored.
- Pinned source: BETA26 `BlockMirror.getBoundingBox` at commit `954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81` returns the 2/16-thick plate bounds for all six facings. Original source: [BlockMirror.java](https://github.com/TheDarkTower314/Thaumcraft-6-Source-Code/blob/954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81/src/main/java/thaumcraft/common/blocks/devices/BlockMirror.java). Event 4 calls `setCollapse(true)` with zero stability cost.

### 2026-11-17 — BETA26 Flux Rift collapse Weakness effect

- Pinned original `EntityFluxRift.completeCollapse()` at `954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81`: both `UNSTABLE` and `VERY_UNSTABLE` fall through to the vanilla Weakness branch. For each living entity in the axis-aligned 32-block query, BETA26 computes `w = (int)((1 - distanceSq / 32.0) * 300)` and applies amplifier 0 for `w * 20` ticks only when `w > 0`. Stable rifts do not receive this Weakness branch; player warp is a separate branch.
- Ported only the exact vanilla Weakness portion in `FluxRiftEntity.completeCollapse`, using the same AABB query, squared-distance formula, integer truncation, amplifier and duration. The custom Flux Taint potion, player warp and guaranteed Void Seed drops remain explicitly unported; no vanilla substitute is used.
- Extended the existing collapse GameTest (no additional test registration): very-unstable and unstable rifts each apply 3000 ticks at distance-squared 16; a target at distance-squared 31.9225 gets no effect because the integer weight truncates to zero; stable collapse does not apply Weakness. The existing required GameTest count remains 12.
- Updated the fail-closed runner to require `TC_GAMETEST_FLUX_RIFT_COLLAPSE_WEAKNESS=PASS`. No global item/entity registry, Gradle, workflow, localization or foreign branch changed. Path overlap checked against open PRs #1–#3 and #5–#32: no collisions for the production entity, GameTest, runner or lane journal paths.

### 2026-11-17 — BETA26 Flux Rift ambient sound: implementation and exact-source asset

- Source comparison against pinned BETA26 `EntityFluxRift.onUpdate` found a missing server behavior: every live Rift calls inherited `Entity.playSound(SoundsTC.evilportal, 0.15 + gaussian*0.066, 0.75 + gaussian*0.1)` when `ticksExisted % 300 == 0`. The modern entity previously never emitted this event.
- Added the byte-identical original `evilportal.ogg` (Git blob `b44b6767fe855520eae55383992510da787d42f4`), registered `thaumcraft:evilportal`, and restored the 300-tick Gaussian volume/pitch call through inherited `Entity.playSound`. This preserves BETA26's default entity sound category (`NEUTRAL`); the original `sounds.json` event category remains `block`, as shipped.
- Added a non-required native GameTest with a fail-closed runner marker: it observes the real `ClientboundSoundEntityPacket`, checks no packet at ticks 299/301, exactly one at 300, original event/entity id, NEUTRAL source, and exact Gaussian volume/pitch for a deterministic seed.
- Added a separate real-client PCM smoke workflow. It launches the actual integrated Minecraft client, summons a live Flux Rift, records PulseAudio PCM around three 300-tick opportunities, and matches the captured waveform against the original OGG; it does not substitute a synthetic sound or server-only assertion.
- Exact-head CI is pending; no M16/M08 score change is claimed.

- Precision correction before acceptance: BETA26 uses a `0.15f` literal promoted to double by the Gaussian expression (exact value `0.15000000596046448`), not a `0.15d` literal. Production, GameTest expected values, and source verifier now preserve that float-to-double promotion exactly.

- Acceptance hygiene: the native GameTest marker is emitted only after `helper.succeed()` and its fake player/recorder/Rift are cleaned up. The generated client smoke now labels its final result as Flux Rift audio rather than inheriting the shared crucible-smoke label.

### 2026-11-17 — Flux Rift sound: compile import and PulseAudio startup blockers corrected

- Fresh repository state: `main` remains `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; own branch before this repair was `4cd456411baa7092ba7cc4247edf03aaa0f54a54`; PR #4 is Draft/Open/Unmerged. - Exact-head Full Port CI [#44169458190](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44169458190) failed at `:compileJava`: `net.minecraft.network.Packet` does not exist in the pinned Minecraft 1.21.1 mappings. The existing compiling Salis GameTest imports `net.minecraft.network.protocol.Packet`; `FluxRiftSoundGameTests` now uses that same package. This is a test-source compile fix, not a production gameplay change.
- Exact-head real-client Rift audio [#44169458199](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44169458199) failed before Minecraft launch. ### 2026-11-17 — account for float precision in the 1.21.1 sound packet at GameTest coordinates

- Re-read main `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56` and the current repository instructions/audit. PR #4 was still open and unmerged at prior head `fa2e8212a2de36bb652aa8e4690ec6f4af6b755b`.

- Native GameTests [#44178276904](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44178276904) isolated the remaining Rift assertion precisely. Expected entity position: `(-3113895.5, -57.5, -1.01065835E7)`; packet position: `(-3113895.5, -57.5, -1.0106584E7)`. The packet's Z equals the Java float conversion of the expected Z at this magnitude (`(float)-10106583.5 == -10106584.0`); the prior comparison incorrectly compared the encoded packet against the unrounded double. The 1.21.1 `ClientboundSoundPacket.LOCATION_ACCURACY` is 8.0, i.e. 1/8-block fixed-point scale ([mapping reference](https://mappings.dev/1.21.1/net/minecraft/network/protocol/game/ClientboundSoundPacket.html)).
- The GameTest now derives the expected packet position by float-rounding each entity coordinate before applying the existing 1/8-block tolerance. It still verifies one target packet at tick 300, none at 299, no repeat at 301, exact NEUTRAL source, and deterministic volume/pitch. No production entity code changed.
- Real-client Rift audio [#44178277078](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44178277078) was still in progress at this entry; its final OpenAL device, sink-input and PCM result must be read before claiming sound parity.
- Changed files: `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/entity/FluxRiftSoundGameTests.java` and this journal. No registries, Gradle files, global localization, or other lane paths changed.
- Fixed score remains **59/100**; M16 **4/6**, M08 **5/9**. No score awarded and no merge. Next: rerun native GameTests against the encoded coordinate and complete the actual-client PulseAudio/PCM check.

### 2026-11-18 — repair the jar-hit smoke preflight's hold-order assertion

- Fresh-read the current main instructions and audit, the closure lane, PR #4, and current open PR list. Main remains `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; PR #4 is Draft/Open/Unmerged. Compare before this entry: 1182 ahead / 0 behind.
- Exact prior-head run [Jar SoundType client #44188479859](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44188479859) failed **before package installation and before Minecraft launch**. Its first failing step was `python tools/verify_jar_soundtype_smoke.py`, line 69.
- Root cause is deterministic in the smoke contract: `hit_section` contains an earlier `sleep 0.80` for the no-hit control before `xdotool mousedown 1`. The assertion used `hit_section.index("sleep 0.80")`, which selected that earlier delay and therefore compared the mouse-down position against a sleep that necessarily precedes it. The generated client script itself has the intended order after the press: mouse-down → fatigue-controlled 0.80s hold → capture marker → damage screenshot → mouse-up.

### 2026-11-18 — guarantee Rift PCM capture cleanup on fail-fast paths

- Reviewing the shared client runner showed its EXIT trap only cleans the Minecraft client, window manager and Xvfb; it does not know about the Rift-specific background `timeout/parec` process. A failed game-time query or a short tick window could therefore leave the audio capture alive until its 420-second timeout.
- Added a Rift-local EXIT trap that first terminates/reaps the capture process and then calls the existing shared `cleanup` function. ### 2026-11-18 — place the Rift sound listener at the variable-range source

- Fresh main remains a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56; own branch before this iteration was 101d2e75e78cca38e91d24c47aa26a19dddd2be1; PR #4 is still Draft/Open/Unmerged. This isolates the failure to the natural event/listener path, but does not yet prove a production packet defect.
- Inspection of the real-client driver found an avoidable 1.414-block listener/source separation: the player was teleported to (2.5,150,11.5), while the Rift was summoned at (3.5,150,12.5). The registered event is created with ` SoundEvent.createVariableRangeEvent `; in 1.21.1 its hearing range is volume-dependent (` getRange(float) `), and the BETA26 source uses a Gaussian volume around 0.15. Thus a low but valid random volume can shrink the audible range below the test's offset. The test was not controlling for this and could report a false negative even when the event was sent correctly.
- Narrow test-only correction: define one set of Rift coordinates and use it for both player teleport and entity summon; log ` RIFT_LISTENER_AT_SOURCE=PASS `; extend the static verifier to require same-coordinate ordering and reject the old offset. Original BETA26 volume/pitch formulas, PCM thresholds, server cadence and production Java remain untouched.
- Commits: driver [9dc52bd48f9f](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/9dc52bd48f9f2365db03c238a66d6ac369c1449c); verifier [7d0397f91906](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/7d0397f9190636968f70a1aa9daf37d25d4eaa8a). Changed paths are only ` tools/client_rift_ambient_sound_smoke.py `, ` tools/verify_rift_ambient_sound.py `, and this lane journal. No Java, sound registry/assets, Gradle, shared CI, main or other workstream branch changed.
- No local Gradle/client/server was launched. Exact-head Rift real-client run [#44228867959](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44228867959), Full Port CI [#44228868044](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44228868044), and Native Aura/Crystal GameTests [#44228867966](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44228867966) were queued when this entry was written. ### 2026-11-18 — client sound-event probe covers both NeoForge event variants

- API/source review found that a Rift's inherited `Entity.playSound` forwards through the Level coordinate-based sound path; NeoForge exposes both `PlayLevelSoundEvent.AtEntity` and `PlayLevelSoundEvent.AtPosition`. Listening only to the former could falsely report that the client never received the sound if the normal path fires the latter ([NeoForge 1.21.1 event API](https://aldak.netlify.app/javadoc/1.21.1-21.1.x/net/neoforged/neoforge/event/playlevelsoundevent)).
- Added the position-event listener alongside the entity-event listener. Both record original/new volume and pitch, source, cancellation state and position. The server due marker now records the source position; the driver accepts client receipt only when position, original Gaussian volume and pitch match the server event, so the explicit `/playsound` positive control (volume/pitch 1/1) cannot satisfy the natural-event gate.
- This is still diagnostic-only. No sound behavior or BETA26 parameters changed. Exact-head checks on the preceding SHA are superseded; new workflow runs on this commit are required.

- Current base `main` remains `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; closure branch code/test HEAD before this journal entry: `a64efda1f6321bb52a01c46a3c37a212afb5591b`. The second inline Python heredoc could not import `scan_rune_log_parser` because it lacked `tools/` in `sys.path`. The same artifact showed that the cow's 63-tick particles all expired, but the iron golem's 120-tick particles had not: the old 4-second wait (plus 0.5-second pre-capture) was shorter than the 6-second BETA26 lifetime. This was a test observation-window defect, not evidence of a production particle defect.

## 2026-11-18 — Salis particle RNG isolation (code committed; exact-head CI pending)

**BETA26 source evidence:** pinned `ItemMagicDust.java` at `954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81` calls `doSparkles` only inside `if (world.isRemote)` after a valid dust trigger. Its 50 hand-trail velocities/delays and exposed-face coordinates/delays are sampled from the client world's RNG. Those cosmetic draws do not advance the authoritative server world's RNG.

**Port defect:** `SalisMundusSparkles.sendToActor` had moved those random draws to the server and used `ServerLevel.random`. A purely visual Salis use therefore consumed authoritative world RNG and could perturb unrelated server-side random outcomes.

**Fix:** `SalisMundusSparkles.java` now creates one detached `RandomSource.create()` per effect and uses it for trail Gaussian velocities, trail delays, exposed-face Gaussian coordinates, and face delays. The visual distributions and actor-only packet routing are unchanged. The client particle provider still uses the client world's RNG, as the original client-side effect did.

**Regression guard:** `tools/test_salis_queue_unload_jvm.py` now requires the detached RNG, rejects `level.random`/`level.getRandom()` in the particle projection, and checks the six Gaussian and two delay call sites. This is a source-level side-effect guard; it is not a substitute for the JVM queue regression or a real client smoke.

**Coordination boundary:** `SalisMundusItem.java` still samples sound pitch and packet seed from the server level RNG. It was deliberately not changed because open PRs #3 and #7 both modify that file. The residual sound-RNG side effect must be handled after that overlap is resolved.

**Verification:** code commit `89c489d7c4509d2527b76211addd82a6896c56d5`. Exact-head Salis queue JVM regression [#44322650226](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44322650226) and Full Port CI [#44322650676](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44322650676) are queued. The previous-head Salis queue JVM regression [#44318074225](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44318074225) passed, but predates the new source guard. Container checkout is unavailable because DNS resolution for github.com fails; no local test or local NeoForge compile is claimed.

**Other exact previous-head evidence:** Thaumometer scan sound [#44318073887](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44318073887), Thaumometer rune client [#44318074189](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44318074189), Flux Rift ambient sound [#44318074098](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44318074098), jar sound [#44318073911](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44318073911), and Aura/Crystal GameTests [#44318073905](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44318073905) passed. Tube-particle smoke [#44318074036](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44318074036) failed before launching Minecraft: Gradle `:downloadAssets` received HTTP 304 for asset `cec25b0246d77e6f1dcef0d051ccb328ba39227a`. Exact-head rerun [#44322650324](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44322650324) is queued; treat the earlier result as an asset-download infrastructure failure, not a gameplay failure.

**Assessment:** no progress points awarded. Fixed rubric remains **59/100**; models/sounds/particles/localization remains **4/6**. The production change is not yet accepted by exact-head CI, NeoForge compilation, or a real client.

**Next action:** first inspect exact-head #44322650226 and #44322650676, then the tube-particle rerun #44322650324. After PR #3/#7 no longer overlap `SalisMundusItem.java`, isolate the remaining sound-packet RNG from the server world RNG. Only then resume the separate two-client Salis routing acceptance.

### 2026-11-18 — Flux Rift listener fixture: eliminate the fall window before platform placement

- Fresh state: main remains a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56; own branch HEAD before this fix was dda326d6ad27821831d2db61da0076f42f5a4c6e. PR #4 is OPEN/DRAFT/UNMERGED. Read the current roadmap, AGENTS, parallel workflow, fixed audit, this lane and current PR changed-file inventory.

It then failed only RIFT_LISTENER_AT_SOURCE=FAIL; the server log shows teleport at 18:04:48.554, successful fill at 18:04:54.807 and platform marker at 18:05:01.125.
- Root cause is in the generated driver, not production Java: generic send_chat() waits four seconds after every command (plus UI/input time). It teleported the player directly to Y=150 before support existed; the player had several seconds to fall before the Y=149 barrier was placed. The block marker therefore passed while the player-position marker failed. This is a concrete fixture-order/timing defect; the sound event itself was not reached.
- Corrected the driver to use a short, ordered send_chat_fast() path for teleporting to Y=300 inside the target chunk and placing the platform, then verify the barrier and teleport to Y=150 only after support exists. The fast helper uses 5 ms typing delay and a 250 ms post-submit gap; the normal four-second helper remains for slower world interactions. On a future listener failure, the driver now requests /data get entity @p Pos before exiting.
- Updated the existing verifier to require the high-teleport → fast-fill → platform-marker → source-teleport order and the bounded fast helper. No workflow, Gradle, production Java, assets, registries, localization, or foreign branch changed.
- Open PRs #1–#3 and #5–#40 were each checked with changed-file inventories; none changes tools/client_rift_ambient_sound_smoke.py or tools/verify_rift_ambient_sound.py. PR #4 is this lane's owner.

## 2026-11-19 — Flux Rift PCM capture isolation (M16 test harness)

- Re-inspected the failed exact-head real-client artifact [#14481497317](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44416947813/artifacts/14481497317). The rift-event.pcm file is **72.14 s** (13,850,880 bytes, 48-kHz stereo s16le). The packaged JAR contains the pinned original BETA26 evilportal.ogg (Git blob b44b6767fe855520eae55383992510da787d42f4), duration **14.524 s**. The old client log records five natural due events at entity ticks **300/600/900/1200/1500**, with different Gaussian pitches 0.5213939, 0.6544856, 0.94563955, 0.81210744, 0.73586583; the listener was at the Rift source (distanceSqr=0.0) and server/client sound-dispatch markers were present.
- The old single-source matcher found correlation=0.1889, baseline=0.0000, offset=39.971 s, pitch=0.5225; the separate /playsound positive control matched at **0.9910** / pitch 1.0. A forensic 14-s prefix near the first natural event correlates at **0.288** at the server-reported pitch; this is not an acceptance test. The capture therefore did **not** isolate one sound: several long pitch-shifted copies overlapped while the matcher compared the whole recording to one copy. This invalidates that PCM result as a clean single-event attribution; it does **not** yet prove the natural playback is correct.
- Root cause is in the smoke fixture, not established production Java: the event recorder started before world/listener setup, the test waited through a 340-game-tick window, and the background recorder was a timeout wrapper whose PID was signalled instead of owning the direct parec process.
- Updated tools/client_rift_ambient_sound_smoke.py: start a direct parec recorder only after the listener is at the measured source and immediately before summoning; record the pre-summon game time/log offset; accept only the first entity tickCount=300 event and require gameTime delta >=300, positive volume, source distance <=1, server call and matching client event. Then /tick freeze, wait **34 s** (14.524 s / minimum matcher pitch 0.45 = 32.3 s maximum nominal clip duration), verify no further tick/due markers and one unique client event signature, and terminate/wait the direct recorder PID. The matcher threshold and pitch search range were not weakened.
- Updated tools/verify_rift_ambient_sound.py to reject the old multi-event wait and timeout-wrapped recorder, and require the first-event/freeze/direct-PID ordering. No production Java, Gradle, workflow YAML, global registry or localization changed.
- Commits: [819c6b7](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/819c6b7acc104d8a276f49a63de51e4880b5472d) (driver), [828ca4f](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/828ca4fd9ddeff536f7f4139f52cc8dfd2ad6a18) (remove stale generator marker guard), [8b96b70](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/8b96b70effad5ebeca8fca80abc67739cd80d9d0) (verifier).
- Intermediate preflight attempts are superseded, not gameplay failures: [#44427335657](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44427335657) on 819c6b7 failed because the generator still required RIFT_LISTENER_STILL_AT_SOURCE=PASS; [#44427385742](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44427385742) on 828ca4f failed because the verifier still required TC_RIFT_LISTENER_STILL_AT_SOURCE. Both stopped at the verifier step; the Minecraft client was **not launched**. The corrected exact-head Flux Rift client run [#44427439793](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44427439793) and Full Port CI [#44427440016](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44427440016) are **queued** at this check. No result is claimed for them.
- Checked all 40 other open PRs (#1–#3, #5–#41) for the two edited tool paths and this lane journal; no path overlap was found. PR [#4](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/pull/4) remains open/Draft/unmerged; the existing SalisMundusItem.java overlap with PR #3 remains a separate merge blocker.

## 2026-11-19 — M08 starvation adjacency no longer joins an in-flight neighbor chunk

- Fresh-read main `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`, current roadmap, AGENTS, parallel workflow, fixed audit and this lane. Working branch before this iteration: `38a2a514a57a7e022fb0186b2bd4ca28d8be96a6`; current code commit: [`0118156a3a3c94730bf02135bc9495b4a8bfc27e`](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/0118156a3a3c94730bf02135bc9495b4a8bfc27e).
- Found a specific inconsistency in `CrystalClusterBlock.randomTick`: the size-zero starvation branch used `hasChunkAt(neighbor)` followed by `getBlockState(neighbor)`. This class already documents that `hasChunkAt` may be true while a neighbor chunk is still in its loading pipeline, and that `getBlockState` can join the pending chunk future. The same hazard had already been removed from the support/outline and spread-destination paths, but remained in this adjacency path.
- Replaced the starvation lookup with the existing non-joining `loadedDestination` helper (`getChunkSource().getChunkNow`). A not-yet-ready neighbor is treated as unknown for this tick; a fully loaded same-variant neighbor still triggers the original BETA26 size-zero removal. No chunk ticket or force-load is requested.
- Added `tools/test_crystal_starvation_neighbor_lookup.py`, a source regression guard that rejects reintroduction of `hasChunkAt`/direct `getBlockState` in this branch and requires the `getChunkNow` adapter. The Aura/Crystal runner executes it before launching the native server tests.
- Changed files:
  - `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/block/CrystalClusterBlock.java`
  - `tools/run_crystal_spread_gametest.sh`
  - `tools/test_crystal_starvation_neighbor_lookup.py`
- No overlap with changed paths in the other 40 open PRs (#1–#3, #5–#41). PR #4's existing `SalisMundusItem.java` overlap with PR #3 is unchanged; this iteration did not touch that file, shared registries, Gradle, workflow YAML, or another branch.
- Native Aura/Crystal GameTests [#44456713307](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44456713307) **SUCCESS** on the exact code SHA. Job log proves:
  - `TC_CRYSTAL_STARVATION_LOOKUP_GUARD=PASS lookup=getChunkNow no_join=YES`

  - `TC_GAMETEST_CRYSTAL_STARVATION_ZERO_TOUCHING_SAME=PASS`
  - all **16 required GameTests passed** in both write and cold-restart read phases; both server JVMs exited and both Gradle phases reported BUILD SUCCESSFUL.
- Full Port CI [#44456707288](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44456707288) **SUCCESS** on the exact code SHA: Core parity **10,046 assertions**, NeoForge build **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**, dedicated server reached `Done (8.792s)`, JAR uploaded as artifact [#14497817430](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44456707288/artifacts/14497817430). ## 2026-11-19 — M08 Flux Rift proximity AABB corrected to BETA26 block-center semantics

- Re-read fresh main (a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56), AGENTS.md, ROADMAP_RU.md, analysis/PARALLEL_WORKFLOW_RU.md, fixed audit, this lane, PR #4 and all 45 open PR file lists. PR #4 remains OPEN/DRAFT/UNMERGED. The two edited paths below have no overlap with PR #1–#3 or #5–#45.
- Compared FluxRiftSpawnEvents.trySpawnRiftEntity against pinned BETA26 EntityUtils.getEntitiesInRange(World, BlockPos, Entity, Class, 32.0). The original centers its AABB at (BlockPos + 0.5) and grows exactly 32 blocks. The port used new AABB(spawnPos).inflate(32), whose faces are shifted outward by 0.5 block; this could reject a valid Rift spawn when an existing Rift occupied only that false-positive shell.
- Production commit [af40287a9e3eddff898514eefbf62ddbbc697494](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/af40287a9e3eddff898514eefbf62ddbbc697494) extracts the source-equivalent centered bounds and uses them in the production nearby-Rift guard.
- Native regression commit [1f141a8eda012cd8cc634c25c443ad920c1da617](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/1f141a8eda012cd8cc634c25c443ad920c1da617) adds usesBETA26CenteredAabbForNearbyRiftGuard: a real FluxRift entity is positioned so its bounds intersect the former shifted query but not the BETA26 query; the production helper must not classify it as nearby. The existing native spawn test continues to cover a genuinely nearby Rift before Flux debit.
- Exact-head workflows on 1f141a8 have been triggered but are QUEUED at this journal update: Full Port CI [#44486394261](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44486394261), Aura/Crystal GameTests [#44486394496](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44486394496), plus the existing M16 client/sound/resource matrix. No compile or GameTest result is claimed yet.
- No registry, Gradle, global CI, SalisMundusItem.java, or foreign branch changed. The new test uses the already registered Flux Rift entity and existing GameTest workflow.
- Fixed rubric remains 59/100, M08 5/9, M16 4/6. No score awarded: this removes one false-positive spawn suppression and does not close the wider Rift/world parity gaps.
- Next: inspect the exact-head Aura/Crystal GameTest and Full Port CI, then resume M16's real-client evidence; the remaining major M16 gate is still two separately launched clients for Salis packet/particle delivery at a chunk boundary and through unload/reload, not another EmbeddedChannel simulation.

It requires both clients to receive positive controls, actor-only target particles and sound, exactly two actor-side chunk-origin packets (pre-unload positive control and post-reload fresh control), zero observer packets for those actor-targeted events, native no-replay markers and final ticket cleanup.

### Two-client harness launch guard follow-up (19 Nov 2026)

- Static review caught a process-selection hazard before accepting the real-client run: the NeoForge DevLaunch main class is also used by the dedicated-server JVM, so identifying the template by that class name alone could clone the server instead of the client. The process detector now requires the explicit forgeclient launch target (or vanilla client main class), while retaining the game-directory requirement.
- Corrected the static contract to assert the literal forgeclient launch-target test. The smoke environment helper also propagates both opt-in system properties and THAUMCRAFT_SMOKE to the server, template and both cloned client JVMs without relying only on workflow-global environment.
- Follow-up commits: [47aa8f9](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/47aa8f96dd3202327e40840511bdb1d2c4ddf846), [f8939d4](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/f8939d4a6d514dc9ea5d7fe109c447334b7d7016), [2e9867b](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/2e9867b495fc1fb6cfac3304c01dc47ba4084810).

The older run [#44495368020](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44495368020) is on the superseded launch-detector version and must not be used as acceptance evidence.
- No runtime result is claimed. The next action remains to inspect #44495779154 from its static-contract step through both real client JVMs, server logs and uploaded packet evidence.


### 1.21.1 Quick Play launch-argument correction (19 Nov 2026)

- Before the first runtime attempt, checked the Minecraft client CLI rather than assuming the legacy launcher flags still exist. Java Edition 1.20 removed --server and --port and replaced them with --quickPlayMultiplayer; the driver now removes legacy flags and sets the server identifier to 127.0.0.1:25565. Source: https://minecraft.wiki/w/Java_Edition_client_command_line_arguments and https://minecraft.wiki/w/Java_Edition_1.20.
- The process detector now selects the explicit forgeclient launch target (or the vanilla client main class), not the shared NeoForge DevLaunch main class. This avoids accidentally cloning the dedicated-server JVM.
- The static contract was updated to guard the 1.21.1 Quick Play argument. These are test-driver corrections only; no gameplay implementation or shared Gradle/CI files changed.

### 2026-11-19 — two-client Salis launcher: Flite narrator dependency identified and fixed

- Inspected the actual failed real-client artifact for [two-client run #44505187888](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44505187888), artifact [14524111372](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44505187888/artifacts/14524111372). The dedicated server reached ready, but the temporary Gradle `runClient` process exited during `GameNarrator` construction. The exact exception is `Narrator$InitializeException: Failed to load library flite`; JNA reports `libflite.so: cannot open shared object file`. The later `RUNCLIENT_TEMPLATE_PROCESS_NOT_FOUND` was only the driver's secondary symptom after that client JVM exited.
- Ubuntu Noble's `flite1-dev` package provides the unversioned `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libflite.so` required by JNA's `NativeLibrary.getInstance("flite")`, and depends on the runtime `libflite1`: [package file list](https://packages.ubuntu.com/eu/noble/amd64/flite1-dev/filelist), [package details](https://packages.ubuntu.com/en/noble/flite1-dev).
- Updated only the isolated `.github/workflows/closure-salis-two-client.yml` to install `flite1-dev` and added an explicit `ctypes.CDLL("libflite.so")` preflight before the real clients. Updated `tools/test_salis_two_client_probe.py` so the dependency and preflight cannot silently disappear. This is a CI-environment fix, not a Thaumcraft gameplay change.
- The exact-head rerun after these changes is required. Until both JVMs actually start and the server/packet/chunk assertions pass, two-client Salis acceptance remains unproven.
- Separate M08 note: the prior native GameTest [#44505188050](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44505188050) failed at `AuraGenerationGameTests.java:339` after the preceding AABB edge test passed. The current fixture adds a blocker and waits one tick, whereas the same file already has a bounded entity-registration poll for the spawned Rift. The exact-head rerun [#44510879796](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44510879796) must be checked before deciding whether this is the same entity-manager visibility race; no speculative production change was made.
- Fixed audit remains **59/100**, M16 **4/6**, M08 **5/9**. No score awarded. Next: inspect the Flite preflight and exact-head two-client run, then handle the M08 GameTest result separately.

### 2026-11-19 — two-client fixture login deadline and ModDev run-directory mismatch corrected

- Fresh-read main HEAD `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`, current `AGENTS.md`, `ROADMAP_RU.md`, parallel workflow and fixed audit. The closure lane remains explicitly assigned to `feature/closure-near-complete`; M06/M07 were not activated and no foreign branch or main was changed. Dedicated server starts; actor client window opens and `TC_SalisActor joined the game`. Observer client also reaches its real window and attempts connection, but never joins. Its log ends with `Client disconnected with reason: Disconnected`.
- The server log provides a deterministic cause: `SalisTwoClientServerProbe.LOGIN_TIMEOUT_TICKS = 1200` is only 60 seconds at 20 TPS. The server starts at 13:16:07Z, actor joins at 13:16:36Z, observer launch begins at 13:16:41Z, and the server marks `SALIS_TWO_CLIENT_FAILED` at 13:17:15Z — immediately before the observer's connection attempt is rejected. This is a test-fixture deadline defect, not evidence of incorrect Salis packet routing.
- The same artifact shows `NoSuchFileException: server.properties`. The driver wrote configuration under `run/server`, but NeoForge ModDevGradle's documented default run directory is the project's `run` subdirectory: https://docs.neoforged.net/toolchain/docs/plugins/mdg/#runs. The test therefore started with default server properties instead of the intended bounded/offline fixture configuration.
- Corrected only the two-client acceptance harness: increased the fixture login deadline from 1200 to 3600 ticks (180 seconds); changed the dedicated-server config/log directory to `ROOT/run`; and added a fail-closed check that the server actually logs `Loading properties` without `Failed to load properties from file: server.properties`. Added static guards for both invariants. No SalisMundusItem production behavior or packet implementation changed.

### 2026-11-20 — исправлен путь клиентской конфигурации для crooked=false

- Установлена конкретная причина предыдущего real-client отказа. В build.gradle run client { client() } не задаёт gameDirectory; run gameTestServer отдельно задаёт run/closure-gametest. Документация ModDevGradle указывает, что default game directory для run — проектный run/ ([Runs](https://docs.neoforged.net/toolchain/docs/plugins/mdg/)). NeoForge ModConfig.Type.CLIENT загружает конфигурацию из client config folder с суффиксом -client ([1.21.1 config docs](https://docs.neoforged.net/docs/1.21.1/misc/config/)).
- Smoke ошибочно записывал thaumcraft-client.toml в run/closure-gametest/config, то есть в каталог отдельного GameTest server, а не runClient. Это объясняет, почему клиент продолжал использовать crooked=true и выводил crooked=-2 для обеих quad, несмотря на записанный fixture false.
- Исправлен только тестовый драйвер: теперь он создаёт run/config/thaumcraft-client.toml до запуска runClient, проверяет значение до старта и в EXIT trap отдельно сообщает JAR_LABEL_CONFIG_EXPECTED_PATH=YES/NO, сохраняя поиск всех найденных config-файлов.
- Source-contract теперь проверяет, что client() остаётся на default project/run directory, а run/closure-gametest относится только к gameTestServer; он отвергает старый путь. Строгие требования к двум маркерам crooked=0 и пороги screenshot 800 paper / 100 glyph не изменены.
- Изменены ровно три файла: tools/client_jar_liquid_smoke.py, tools/test_client_jar_liquid_probe.py, этот lane journal. Производственный Java, build.gradle, общие smoke scripts, общие CI, registry и локализации не менялись.
- Пересечение проверено по всем 50 другим открытым PR (#1–#3 и #5–#51): ни один не меняет эти три пути. PR #4 — собственная ветка этого направления; известный конфликт PR #3/#4 в SalisMundusItem.java не затронут.
- Предыдущий exact-head jar run [#44670846597](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44670846597) был запущен до исправления и не считается доказательством нового пути. Следующий exact-head run должен пройти Core source-contract, затем реальный клиент должен показать оба crooked=0, сохранить 100-jar-aspect-label.png и пройти прежние pixel gates.

## 2026-11-20 — M16: pedestal display rotation uses the wrong clock

### BETA26 comparison
Pinned source reference: `TheDarkTower314/Thaumcraft-6-Source-Code` commit `954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81`, `TilePedestalRenderer.java`.
The original renderer computes `ticksExisted + partialTicks` from the render-view entity. The NeoForge port previously used `Level.getGameTime()`; those clocks diverge for a client joining an already-running multiplayer world, so the displayed item can begin at a different rotation.

### Change
- Added `LegacyPedestalRotationAngle` as a small core parity helper.
- Changed `PedestalItemRenderer` to use `Minecraft.getInstance().getCameraEntity().tickCount` plus partial tick, with a null-camera fallback.
- Added five angle boundary cases and a source contract to `CoreParityTest`; the contract rejects a return to `getGameTime()`.
- No registries, Gradle files, global localization, or workflow definitions changed.

### Exact-head M16 gate before this change
On `ad79fc28a3ec667964efb0493149acb664229987`, all 23 returned PR workflow runs completed successfully after retrying two jobs that had initially failed during external Gson dependency resolution. The retry reached the real client:
- Workbench RU/EN GUI: four locale/scale matrix cells passed (the last RU scale-2 cell was still running when first inspected; it later completed successfully).
- Six crystal placement variants: real-client original PCM match passed for aer, ignis, terra, ordo, perditio, vitium.
- Crystal break: real-client block break and original PCM match passed.
- Crystal outline: real-client block-picking smoke passed.
- Full Port and Aura/Crystal GameTests passed.
The two initial failures were transient dependency-resolution failures, not gameplay failures; both retries completed successfully.

### Acceptance state
This corrects a concrete BETA26 client-rendering discrepancy. Core formula/source-contract and the new full-port CI run must pass on the new commit before this change is accepted. A dedicated pedestal screenshot smoke is still desirable; it is not claimed by the source-contract test. M16 remains 4/6 and is not closed. Overall index remains 59/100.

### Next action
Verify the new commit's Core parity and Full Port result. Then add/execute a real-client pedestal display smoke if the existing client harness can host it without modifying shared Gradle or global workflow configuration. Continue M16 only on independently owned visual/audio gaps; do not enter research, alchemy, or infusion feature branches.

## 2026-11-20 — M16 Flux Rift: restore BETA26 blend/depth split

- Fresh refs before editing: main `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; PR #4 head `781256b052dd9fdfa6204728bb85c39914659905`, OPEN/DRAFT/UNMERGED, 1433 commits ahead and 0 behind. Re-read main `AGENTS.md`, `ROADMAP_RU.md`, `analysis/PARALLEL_WORKFLOW_RU.md`, and the fixed audit. Other open PRs #1–#3 and #5–#55 were checked for the three target paths; no overlaps.
- Pinned BETA26 source `RenderFluxRift.doRender` at source commit `954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81`: q=0..2 call `glDepthMask(false)` and `glBlendFunc(770,1)` (SRC_ALPHA/ONE); q=3 restores depth writes and uses `glBlendFunc(770,771)` (SRC_ALPHA/ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA). The port previously submitted all four layers through `RenderType.lightning()`, making the core additive and depth-write-disabled too.
- Production correction: `FluxRiftRenderer` now submits three outer layers through a POSITION_COLOR lightning-shader render type with LIGHTNING_TRANSPARENCY and COLOR_WRITE, then the core through a POSITION_COLOR render type with TRANSLUCENT_TRANSPARENCY and COLOR_DEPTH_WRITE. Both retain LEQUAL depth testing, no culling, and no texture. The original end-shader/star compositing and goggles-only depth bypass remain explicitly unported.
- Added smoke-only per-layer markers. The existing isolated real-client Rift workflow now requires exactly four markers (3 additive/depth-write-off, 1 alpha/depth-write-on) and captures the actual client screenshot after the renderer has run. The existing verifier locks the production state and runtime marker contract. ## 2026-11-20 — M16 Flux Rift ambient smoke: valid negative Gaussian roll made the test flaky

### BETA26 source finding
- Pinned original EntityFluxRift.onUpdate at source commit 954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81, line 273, computes (float)(0.15000000596046448 + rand.nextGaussian() * 0.066) and (float)(0.75 + rand.nextGaussian() * 0.1). The port uses the same float-equivalent base and Gaussian coefficients.
- The failed exact-head client run #44750634247 observed volume -0.02249552 at listener distance squared 0.0. That is a valid tail sample of the original Gaussian formula, not a production defect; negative volume naturally yields no eligible audible player. Do not clamp or alter normal gameplay randomness.
- The subsequent real-client run #44757919775 passed on the same production code: render-layer selection 3 additive / 1 alpha, depth writes outer-off/core-on, tick 300, volume 0.22603346, pitch 0.82691324, server sound call, client sound event, and original evilportal.ogg PCM correlation 0.5459. This confirms the prior failure was nondeterministic test input, not a reproducible renderer/audio defect.

### Deterministic smoke-only correction
- The entity now finds a deterministic seed whose BETA26 Gaussian sample is audibly positive, but uses it only when THAUMCRAFT_SMOKE=1. Normal gameplay continues to consume the entity's existing random source unchanged; no volume clamp or formula change is made.
- The real-client driver requires the deterministic-seed marker before accepting the positive-volume event. The native sound GameTest shares the same seed selector and verifies repeatability plus valid volume/pitch bounds.
- Changed files: FluxRiftEntity.java, FluxRiftSoundGameTests.java, tools/client_rift_ambient_sound_smoke.py, tools/verify_rift_ambient_sound.py, and this journal. No registry, Gradle, shared workflow, global localization, or other feature branch changed.
- Exact pre-change branch head: 7e45cfd66e4f2c0d5379ba780d43391b3db73dfc; main remains a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56; PR #4 remains OPEN/DRAFT/UNMERGED. All open PRs #1–#3 and #5–#56 were checked for these target paths; no overlap was found.
- New exact-head CI is pending. Required evidence: source verifier, native Flux Rift sound GameTest, Full Port, and a fresh real Minecraft client with deterministic seed marker plus actual PCM match. The earlier #44757919775 pass remains valid evidence for the original unforced gameplay path, but it does not test the new deterministic smoke instrumentation.
- Fixed audit remains 59/100, M16 4/6, M08 5/9. No point awarded and no category closed.

### Static verifier false positive — 20.11.2026

- Fresh state: `main` HEAD `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; own branch `feature/closure-near-complete`; PR [#4](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/pull/4) remains OPEN/DRAFT/UNMERGED. - Exact-head real-client run [#44813125979](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44813125979) launched Minecraft 1.21.1, created an allow-commands creative/peaceful world, initialized the PulseAudio backend, and matched the original `evilportal.ogg` positive control at correlation `0.9967`. The client log explicitly confirms `Teleported Dev to 3.500000, 300.000000, 12.500000`, but the following marker `TC_RIFT_HIGH_TELEPORT_PRESENT` was absent. The natural Rift was not summoned and no ambient-event PCM was captured.
- Root cause in the smoke driver: after a successful teleport to Y=300, the player is already falling when the marker command is evaluated; its selector required `distance=..1` from the exact Y=300 point. That predicate can become false before the command arrives. This is a test-driver false negative, not evidence of a production Rift/audio defect.
- Generator fix: the high marker now selects the target X/Z column and Y=150..400 (`x=3,y=150,z=12,dx=1,dy=250,dz=1`), which excludes the observed normal spawn at Y=73 while tolerating the fall. The barrier fill now uses the reliable `send_chat` path: its one-second pre-submit delay is short relative to the 150-block fall; the four-second wait is after the fill command has executed.
- Files changed: `tools/client_rift_ambient_sound_smoke.py` and `tools/verify_rift_ambient_sound.py`. Commits: [`5d85404`](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/5d854049297804d5af77f4efb4ae8436d79e3fb7), [`cf8c4b5`](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/cf8c4b58bb99c0ae642157b723b5b3aeee056007). The first post-fix run [#44826566315](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44826566315) failed before Minecraft launch because the verifier still expected the old fast-fill string (line 126); no gameplay evidence was produced. - On the corrected SHA, real-client Rift run [#44826743747](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44826743747) and Full Port [#44826743798](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44826743798) are **QUEUED**; Aura/Crystal and the remaining M16 client workflows are also queued. ## 2026-11-21 — revert the non-parity frozen-daylight aura change

- Re-read the pinned Thaumcraft 6.1.BETA26 source at revision [954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81](https://github.com/TheDarkTower314/Thaumcraft-6-Source-Code/tree/954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81). In `src/main/java/thaumcraft/common/world/aura/AuraThread.java`, `run()` calls `processAuraChunk` only inside `if (lastWorldTime != world.getWorldTime())`; the thread then sleeps for approximately one second. Kept the 20-server-tick scheduler throttle as the modern event-loop approximation of AuraThread's one-second wake, but processing now also requires a changed world time. Restored the source-era world-time predicate assertion, retained explicit scheduler-boundary checks, removed the contradictory frozen-daytime test, and updated the required GameTest markers.
- Changed paths: `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/world/ThaumcraftAuraWorldEvents.java`, `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/world/AuraGenerationGameTests.java`, `tools/run_crystal_spread_gametest.sh`, and this journal. ## 2026-11-21 — preserve BETA26 per-instance Flux Phage curative items

## 2026-11-21 — M08 crystal support: BETA26 polished stone variants

- Fresh main HEAD: `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`. Lane code HEAD: `55770ed2cedbe32764c9eb5a7401b522df2680e3`; PR #4 remains OPEN/DRAFT/UNMERGED. Branch is 1533 commits ahead and 0 behind main; no rebase, force-push or merge.

- BETA26 source at pinned `954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81`: `BlockUtils.isBlockTouching(world, pos, Material.ROCK, true)` accepts any adjacent block whose material is ROCK and whose touching face is solid. In 1.12.2, polished granite/diorite/andesite are metadata variants of `minecraft:stone` (IDs 1:2, 1:4, 1:6), so they share BlockStone's ROCK material. The port's data-driven `crystal_supports` tag omitted their 1.21.1 block IDs, incorrectly rejecting all three.
- Production fix: add `minecraft:polished_granite`, `minecraft:polished_diorite`, `minecraft:polished_andesite` to `src/main/resources/data/thaumcraft/tags/block/crystal_supports.json`. Native regression added to `CrystalSpreadGameTests.polishedStoneSupportsCrystalsLikeBETA26`: actual `hasCrystalSupport` and `BlockState.canSurvive` accept all three; glass and gold remain rejected as non-ROCK controls.
- Exact-code Full Port push [#44929648937](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44929648937) completed SUCCESS: Core parity, NeoForge build, dedicated-server smoke and JAR upload all passed. Re-run attempt 2 completed SUCCESS; the log reports `All 21 required tests passed` and `TC_GAMETEST_CRYSTAL_POLISHED_STONE_SUPPORT=PASS variants=3 glass_rejected=YES gold_rejected=YES`. Artifact: [14717471371](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44929648962/artifacts/14717471371). PR-triggered copies #44929654439/#44929654451 were still queued at the time of this entry; push CI is the accepted exact-head evidence.

## 2026-11-22 — GameTest exposed Wisp sound category regression

- Exact-head Full Port CI on `612e5aac332a9888aa5c194ea00299b53deadc82` passed (NeoForge compile, JAR and dedicated-server smoke): [push #44979798127](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44979798127), [PR #44979802338](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44979802338).
- Native GameTests on the same SHA reached the new Wisp test and observed one real outbound `zap` sound packet at volume `1.0`, pitch `1.1`, but failed two assertions: its category was `NEUTRAL` and the test's `0.125)-block position tolerance was too strict for the positional sound packet. The failure is in [#44979798134](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/44979798134); no Wisp sound GameTest PASS is claimed.
- Root cause of category mismatch: modern `Entity.getSoundSource()` defaults to `NEUTRAL`; merely implementing `Enemy` does not make a `FlyingMob` behave like `Monster`, which explicitly overrides the source to `HOSTILE`. BETA26 `EntityWisp` is an `IMob`, so its sounds belong to the hostile category. Added an explicit `getSoundSource() = HOSTILE` override and made the GameTest assert both the getter and outbound packet category.
- Relaxed only the positional packet tolerance to one block and included both packet and Wisp coordinates in failure diagnostics. The packet is positional (not entity-attached) in this runtime; the test still rejects a sound dispatched more than one block away. Volume and pitch assertions remain exact.
- Next: require Full Port and Native GameTests on the isolated-registration head, especially `TC_GAMETEST_WISP_SOUND=PASS`; then run an actual client audio capture for the three ambient variants, lava-extinguish hurt, death and zap. Fixed rubric remains **59/100**, M08 **5/9**, M16 **4/6**.

## 2026-11-22 — restore BETA26 Wisp vertical damping on both logical sides

- Fresh main HEAD remained `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; this branch was `81d8989937b71499abb19868b7279d6e9ae2383e` before the code change. Re-read main `AGENTS.md`, `ROADMAP_RU.md`, `analysis/PARALLEL_WORKFLOW_RU.md`, the fixed audit and this lane. PR #4 remains OPEN/DRAFT/UNMERGED.
- Compared pinned BETA26 `EntityWisp.onUpdate()` at source revision `954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81`: it multiplies vertical motion by the widened float coefficient `0.6000000238418579` on both logical sides, after base entity update and before the separate `onLivingUpdate()` AI/attack path.
- The port returned on the client before applying damping, so client-side Wisp motion skipped this source behavior. On the server it applied damping only after `tickServer()`, reversing the original ordering, and used `0.6D`, which is not the same IEEE-754 value.
- Moved the shared damping immediately after `super.tick()`, before the client-side return and before server-only AI. The helper uses the exact BETA26 coefficient and leaves X/Z unchanged.
- Added native GameTest `TC_GAMETEST_WISP_VERTICAL_DAMPING` to assert exact double bits for positive/negative Y velocity and unchanged horizontal components. This test exercises the same helper used by both logical sides; it is not a real-client movement screenshot or Wisp zap visual acceptance.
- Changed files: `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/entity/WispEntity.java`, `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/entity/WispEntityGameTests.java`, and this lane journal. - Checked the two Wisp paths against every other currently open PR #1–#3 and #5–#57; no overlaps were found. The lane-journal path was also checked against PRs #2, #3, #5–#9 and #57; no overlap. No shared registry, Gradle, workflow YAML, global localization, main branch or foreign branch changed.

The old line-count boundary was also captured before sending the teleport, so a callback emitted while the command was in flight could be misclassified as post-aim.

The driver now snapshots the log line count after the server's successful teleport response and accepts only renderer callbacks appended after that boundary. The pixel-visibility gate remains unchanged and mandatory; this does not assert that the Wisp is visible.
- Added offline regression assertions for the per-tick probe contract and strict teleport confirmation → post-aim log boundary → fresh renderer callback → screenshot ordering. ### 2026-11-22 — correct Wisp smoke command context and keep the player on the fixture

- Inspected exact-head real-client run [#45070271796](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/45070271796), artifact [14772724334](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/45070271796/artifacts/14772724334). The screenshot still contains sky/platform with no Wisp. New telemetry shows player `(7.1047, 148.9030, 12.4948)`, yaw `-94.08°`, pitch `-26.92°`; Wisp `(8.5210,149.8462,12.1902)`; first post-aim view dot is only **0.9001**, then falls to 0.8262, 0.6436 and 0.5777 while player Y falls to 143.98. The player was teleported west of the fixture's X=8..12 platform and fell; the screenshot is taken from below/alongside the platform. This is a test-driver positioning/rotation defect, not proof of a production renderer failure.
- Root cause: `execute at @e[wisp]` changes command execution position and rotation to the Wisp while leaving the player as executor. The following `tp @p ... facing entity @e[wisp]` therefore calculates facing from the Wisp command context toward the Wisp itself, not from the player's eyes. Minecraft command semantics document that `at` changes execution position/rotation but not executor, and `facing` changes execution rotation from that context: [Minecraft execute command reference](https://minecraft.fandom.com/wiki/Commands/execute), [TeleportCommand 1.21.1 API](https://maven.fabricmc.net/docs/yarn-1.21.1%2Bbuild.3/net/minecraft/server/command/TeleportCommand.html).
- Fixed the isolated client driver in two server-confirmed steps: (1) teleport the player to the live Wisp's east side with `~1.5` so they remain on the existing platform; (2) execute **as the player, at the player, anchored eyes**, face the live Wisp's eyes, and teleport the player to the same position to apply that rotation. The post-aim log boundary is now captured only after both successful teleport responses. No production Wisp movement, rendering, networking, hit mechanics, pixel thresholds or resources were changed.
- Updated offline regression to require the exact position → position confirmation → player-eye facing → rotation confirmation → fresh renderer → screenshot order, and reject the previous self-origin facing command.
- Prior exact-head verification on `9ba9248`: Full Port CI [#45070271792](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/45070271792) **SUCCESS** (NeoForge build, JAR and dedicated-server smoke); Wisp geometry [#45070277461](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/45070277461) **SUCCESS**; Wisp real-client [#45070271796](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/45070271796) **FAILED** at the genuine screenshot gate. A duplicate push-triggered Wisp run [#45070277408](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/45070277408) was still in progress at the time of this edit. All 57 other open PRs were checked for the three paths planned here; no overlap.
- This change is not yet validated. The exact new-head offline Python regression, NeoForge build and real-client Wisp screenshot must run after publication. Fixed audit remains **59/100**, M16 **4/6**, M08 **5/9**; no points awarded. Next: inspect the new `aimDot` and screenshot; only after the Wisp is centered proceed to real melee hit, two-entity payload and three-tick FXBolt expiry.
